+version: 2
+updates:
+  # Keep GitHub Actions at the latest versions.
+  - package-ecosystem: github-actions
+    directory: /
+    schedule:
+      interval: weekly
+  # Keep Rust dependencies current (including breaking major bumps).
+  - package-ecosystem: cargo
+    directory: /
+    schedule:
+      interval: weekly
+    groups:
+      minor-and-patch:
+        update-types:
+          - "minor"
+          - "patch"
diff --git a/.github/workflows/ci.yml b/.github/workflows/ci.yml
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..924b75f
--- /dev/null
+++ b/.github/workflows/ci.yml
@@ -0,0 +1,101 @@
+name: CI
+
+on:
+  push:
+    branches: [main]
+  pull_request:
+
+env:
+  CARGO_TERM_COLOR: always
+  # Raise this towards 100 as coverage grows. The coverage job fails when
+  # total line coverage drops below this number.
+  COVERAGE_THRESHOLD: "55"
+
+jobs:
+  fmt:
+    name: Formatting
+    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
+    steps:
+      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
+      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
+        with:
+          components: rustfmt
+      - name: Check formatting
+        run: cargo fmt --all -- --check
+
+  clippy:
+    name: Clippy (pedantic, zero allowances)
+    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
+    steps:
+      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
+      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
+        with:
+          components: clippy
+      - uses: Swatinem/rust-cache@v2
+      # The workspace denies clippy::pedantic via [workspace.lints.clippy];
+      # -D warnings escalates every remaining warning to an error.
+      # No #[allow] suppression is permitted anywhere in the codebase.
+      - name: Run clippy
+        run: cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
+
+  test:
+    name: Tests (${{ matrix.os }})
+    needs: [fmt, clippy]
+    strategy:
+      fail-fast: false
+      matrix:
+        os: [ubuntu-latest, macos-latest, windows-latest]
+    runs-on: ${{ matrix.os }}
+    steps:
+      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
+      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
+      - uses: Swatinem/rust-cache@v2
+      - name: Run test suite
+        run: cargo test --workspace
+
+  coverage:
+    name: Coverage
+    needs: [test]
+    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
+    steps:
+      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
+      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
+        with:
+          components: llvm-tools-preview
+      - uses: Swatinem/rust-cache@v2
+      - name: Install cargo-llvm-cov
+        uses: taiki-e/install-action@v2
+        with:
+          tool: cargo-llvm-cov
+      - name: Generate coverage report
+        run: |
+          cargo llvm-cov --workspace --lcov --output-path lcov.info \
+            --fail-under-lines "$COVERAGE_THRESHOLD"
+      - name: Print coverage summary
+        run: cargo llvm-cov --workspace --summary-only
+      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
+        with:
+          name: coverage-report
+          path: lcov.info
+
+  build:
+    name: Release build (${{ matrix.os }})
+    needs: [test]
+    strategy:
+      fail-fast: false
+      matrix:
+        os: [ubuntu-latest, macos-latest, windows-latest]
+    runs-on: ${{ matrix.os }}
+    steps:
+      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
+      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
+      - uses: Swatinem/rust-cache@v2
+      - name: Build release binaries
+        run: cargo build --release --workspace
+      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
+        with:
+          name: patch-ts-${{ matrix.os }}
+          path: |
+            target/release/patch-ts
+            target/release/patch-ts.exe
+          if-no-files-found: ignore

diff --git a/.github/workflows/release.yml b/.github/workflows/release.yml
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..d7707e9
--- /dev/null
+++ b/.github/workflows/release.yml
@@ -0,0 +1,139 @@
+name: Release
+
+on:
+  push:
+    tags: ["v*"]
+
+permissions:
+  contents: write
+
+env:
+  CARGO_TERM_COLOR: always
+
+jobs:
+  changelog:
+    name: Generate changelog with git-cliff
+    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
+    outputs:
+      content: ${{ steps.changelog.outputs.content }}
+    steps:
+      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
+        with:
+          fetch-depth: 0
+      # orhun/git-cliff-action@v4 runs git-cliff with the repository's
+      # cliff.toml and emits the latest release section as `steps.changelog.outputs.content`.
+      - name: Generate latest changelog section
+        id: changelog
+        uses: orhun/git-cliff-action@v4
+        with:
+          config: cliff.toml
+          args: -vv --latest --strip header
+        env:
+          OUTPUT: CHANGES.md
+          GITHUB_REPO: ${{ github.repository }}
+      - name: Generate full changelog file
+        uses: orhun/git-cliff-action@v4
+        with:
+          config: cliff.toml
+          args: -vv --unreleased
+        env:
+          OUTPUT: CHANGELOG.md
+          GITHUB_REPO: ${{ github.repository }}
+      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
+        with:
+          name: changelog
+          path: CHANGELOG.md
+
+  build:
+    name: Build ${{ matrix.target }}
+    needs: [changelog]
+    strategy:
+      fail-fast: false
+      matrix:
+        include:
+          - target: x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu
+            os: ubuntu-latest
+            archive: patch-ts-x86_64-linux.tar.gz
+          - target: aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu
+            os: ubuntu-latest
+            archive: patch-ts-aarch64-linux.tar.gz
+          - target: x86_64-apple-darwin
+            os: macos-latest
+            archive: patch-ts-x86_64-macos.tar.gz
+          - target: aarch64-apple-darwin
+            os: macos-latest
+            archive: patch-ts-aarch64-macos.tar.gz
+          - target: x86_64-pc-windows-msvc
+            os: windows-latest
+            archive: patch-ts-x86_64-windows.zip
+    runs-on: ${{ matrix.os }}
+    steps:
+      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
+      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
+        with:
+          targets: ${{ matrix.target }}
+      - uses: Swatinem/rust-cache@v2
+        with:
+          key: ${{ matrix.target }}
+      - name: Install cross-compilation linker
+        if: matrix.target == 'aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu'
+        run: |
+          sudo apt-get update
+          sudo apt-get install -y gcc-aarch64-linux-gnu
+          echo "CARGO_TARGET_AARCH64_UNKNOWN_LINUX_GNU_LINKER=aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
+      - name: Build release binary
+        run: cargo build --release --target ${{ matrix.target }} -p patch-ts
+      - name: Package (unix)
+        if: runner.os != 'Windows'
+        run: |
+          mkdir -p dist
+          cp "target/${{ matrix.target }}/release/patch-ts" dist/
+          cp README.md LICENSE CHANGELOG.md dist/ 2>/dev/null || true
+          tar -czf "${{ matrix.archive }}" -C dist .
+          shasum -a 256 "${{ matrix.archive }}" > "${{ matrix.archive }}.sha256"
+      - name: Package (windows)
+        if: runner.os == 'Windows'
+        shell: pwsh
+        run: |
+          New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path dist | Out-Null
+          Copy-Item "target/${{ matrix.target }}/release/patch-ts.exe" dist/
+          Copy-Item README.md dist/ -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
+          Copy-Item LICENSE dist/ -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
+          Compress-Archive -Path dist/* -DestinationPath "${{ matrix.archive }}"
+          (Get-FileHash "${{ matrix.archive }}" -Algorithm SHA256).Hash | Out-File "${{ matrix.archive }}.sha256"
+      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
+        with:
+          name: release-${{ matrix.target }}
+          path: |
+            ${{ matrix.archive }}
+            ${{ matrix.archive }}.sha256
+
+  release:
+    name: Publish GitHub release
+    needs: [changelog, build]
+    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
+    steps:
+      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
+      - uses: actions/download-artifact@v4
+        with:
+          path: dist
+          merge-multiple: true
+      - name: Create GitHub release
+        uses: softprops/action-gh-release@v3
+        with:
+          body: ${{ needs.changelog.outputs.content }}
+          files: |
+            dist/*.tar.gz
+            dist/*.zip
+            dist/*.sha256
+
+  publish:
+    name: Publish to crates.io
+    needs: [release]
+    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
+    steps:
+      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
+      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
+      - name: Publish patch-ts to crates.io
+        run: cargo publish --token ${{ secrets.CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN }}
+        continue-on-error: true

 If you add a new feature, **add tests that cover the happy path, error conditions, and edge cases**. For CLI commands, use `assert_cmd::Command::cargo_bin("patch-ts")` to invoke the binary.
 
+### Coverage
+
+Line coverage is measured with `cargo-llvm-cov`:
+
+```bash
+just coverage        # terminal summary
+just coverage-lcov   # lcov.info for CI/tooling
+```
+
+CI enforces a minimum line-coverage threshold via the `COVERAGE_THRESHOLD`
+environment variable in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — raise it as coverage
+grows; the goal is 100%.
+
+---
+
+## CI / CD
+
+- **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`** — formatting, clippy (pedantic denied
+  workspace-wide, zero allowances), tests on Linux/macOS/Windows, coverage
+  gate, and release builds.
+- **`.github/workflows/release.yml`** — triggered by `v*` tags: generates the
+  changelog with **git-cliff** (`cliff.toml`), builds release binaries for
+  Linux (x86_64 + aarch64), macOS (x86_64 + aarch64) and Windows, attaches
+  them to a GitHub release, and publishes to crates.io.
+- **`.github/workflows/publish.yml`** — publishes to crates.io on published
+  releases.
+- **`.github/dependabot.yml`** — weekly GitHub Actions and Cargo updates.
+
+### Changelog
+
+Commit messages follow [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/)
+(`feat:`, `fix:`, `perf:`, `docs:`, `test:`, `refactor:`, `chore:`).
+The changelog is generated by git-cliff; release notes come from the latest
+section it produces.
+
 ---

 ---
 
+## 🦀 Library Usage (in‑memory API)
+
+`patch-ts` is not only a CLI: the `patch_ts::api` module exposes the core
+engines over plain strings so embedders (editors, bots, AI agents) never need
+to round‑trip content through temporary files.
+
+```rust
+use patch_ts::api;
+use patch_ts::ast::PythonLanguage;
+
+// Unique, ambiguity‑refusing search/replace.
+let patched = api::apply_search_replace("let a = 1;\n", "let a = 1;", "let a = 42;")?;
+
+// Repair unbalanced delimiters in memory (same engine as `balance`).
+let mut lang = PythonLanguage::new();
+let fixed = api::balance_content("x = (1 + 2\n", &mut lang, 3)?;
+assert_eq!(fixed.repaired, "x = (1 + 2)\n");
+
+// Validate without repairing.
+let report = api::validate_content("def f(:\n", &mut lang);
+assert!(!report.valid);
+
+// Build a language backend from a bare extension.
+let mut lang = api::language_from_extension("rs")?;
+```
+
+---
+
 ## 📖 Commands
 
 ### `patch`

+# Changelog
+
+All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

+    ## [Unreleased]

diff --git a/src/api.rs b/src/api.rs

index 0000000..eb33d36

@@ -0,0 +1,276 @@
+//! High-level, in-memory API for library consumers.
+//!
+//! Every other patching entry point in this crate is file-based; this module
+//! exposes the core engines — literal search/replace, delimiter balancing and
+//! syntax validation — over plain strings, so embedders (editors, bots, AI
+//! agents) never need to round-trip content through temporary files.
+//!
+//! # Examples
+//!
+//! ```
+//! use patch_ts::api;
+//! use patch_ts::ast::PythonLanguage;
+//!
+//! // Unique search/replace over raw content.
+//! let patched = api::apply_search_replace("let a = 1;\nlet b = 2;\n", "let a = 1;", "let a = 42;").unwrap();
+//! assert_eq!(patched, "let a = 42;\nlet b = 2;\n");
+//!
+//! // Repair unbalanced delimiters in memory.
+//! let mut lang = PythonLanguage::new();
+//! let fixed = api::balance_content("x = (1 + 2\n", &mut lang, 3).unwrap();
+//! assert_eq!(fixed.repaired, "x = (1 + 2)\n");
+//!
+//! // Validate without repairing.
+//! let mut lang = PythonLanguage::new();
+//! let report = api::validate_content("def f(:\n", &mut lang);
+//! assert!(!report.valid);
+//! ```
+use crate::ast::{DelimiterError, Language};
+use crate::repair::search::minimum_cost_repair;
+use anyhow::{Context, Result};
+
+/// The result of a successful [`balance_content`] call.
+#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub struct BalanceOutcome {
+    /// The repaired source text.
+    pub repaired: String,
+    /// Number of delimiter edits (insertions + deletions) applied.
+    pub actions: usize,
+    /// Total cost of the applied repair sequence.
+    pub cost: usize,
+}
+
+/// The result of a [`validate_content`] call.
+#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
+pub struct SyntaxReport {
+    /// Whether the content parses without ERROR or MISSING nodes.
+    pub valid: bool,
+    /// Delimiter-level problems found by the repair engine.
+    pub delimiter_errors: Vec<DelimiterError>,
+}
+
+/// Replace the unique occurrence of `search` in `content` with `replace`.
+///
+/// # Errors
+///
+/// Returns an error when `search` is empty, or when it matches zero or
+/// several locations — ambiguity is refused rather than guessed at, which is
+/// the safe default for automated patching.
+pub fn apply_search_replace(content: &str, search: &str, replace: &str) -> Result<String> {
+    if search.is_empty() {
+        anyhow::bail!("search text must not be empty");
+    }
+    let matches = content.matches(search).count();
+    match matches {
+        0 => anyhow::bail!("search text not found: {search:?} ({} bytes)", search.len()),
+        1 => Ok(content.replacen(search, replace, 1)),
+        n => anyhow::bail!(
+            "search text matches {n} locations; refine the context or use apply_search_replace_all"
+        ),
+    }
+}
+
+/// Replace every occurrence of `search` in `content` with `replace`,
+/// returning the new content and the number of replacements.
+///
+/// # Errors
+///
+/// Returns an error when `search` is empty.
+pub fn apply_search_replace_all(
+    content: &str,
+    search: &str,
+    replace: &str,
+) -> Result<(String, usize)> {
+    if search.is_empty() {
+        anyhow::bail!("search text must not be empty");
+    }
+    let count = content.matches(search).count();
+    Ok((content.replace(search, replace), count))
+}
+
+/// Balance unbalanced delimiters in `content` in memory.
+///
+/// Runs the same minimum-cost repair search as the `balance` command, but
+/// without touching the filesystem.
+///
+/// # Errors
+///
+/// Returns an error when the content has syntax errors that are not delimiter
+/// problems, or when no repair exists within `max_cost` edits.
+pub fn balance_content(
+    content: &str,
+    language: &mut dyn Language,
+    max_cost: usize,
+) -> Result<BalanceOutcome> {
+    let parse_result = language.parse(content);
+    if language.is_valid(&parse_result) {
+        return Ok(BalanceOutcome {
+            repaired: content.to_string(),
+            actions: 0,
+            cost: 0,
+        });
+    }
+    let errors = language.find_delimiter_errors(&parse_result);
+    if errors.is_empty() {
+        anyhow::bail!("content has syntax errors that are not delimiter problems");
+    }
+    let Some((repaired, actions, cost)) = minimum_cost_repair(content, &errors, language, max_cost)
+    else {
+        anyhow::bail!("unable to balance content within max_cost {max_cost}");
+    };
+    Ok(BalanceOutcome {
+        repaired,
+        actions: actions.len(),
+        cost,
+    })
+}
+
+/// Validate `content` without repairing it.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn validate_content(content: &str, language: &mut dyn Language) -> SyntaxReport {
+    let parse_result = language.parse(content);
+    let valid = language.is_valid(&parse_result);
+    let delimiter_errors = if valid {
+        Vec::new()
+    } else {
+        language.find_delimiter_errors(&parse_result)
+    };
+    SyntaxReport {
+        valid,
+        delimiter_errors,
+    }
+}
+
+/// Build a language backend from a file extension such as `"rs"` or `"py"`.
+///
+/// Thin convenience wrapper over [`crate::ast::detect_language`] for callers
+/// that hold an extension rather than a path.
+///
+/// # Errors
+///
+/// Returns an error for unsupported extensions.
+pub fn language_from_extension(extension: &str) -> Result<Box<dyn Language>> {
+    crate::ast::detect_language(std::path::Path::new(&format!("inline.{extension}")))
+        .with_context(|| format!("unsupported extension {extension:?}"))
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    use super::*;
+    use crate::ast::{DelimiterError, PythonLanguage, RustLanguage, Span};
+
+    #[test]
+    fn search_replace_unique() {
+        let out = apply_search_replace("a\nb\na\n", "b", "B").unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(out, "a\nB\na\n");
+    }
+
+    #[test]

+        assert!(err.to_string().contains("not found"));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn search_replace_ambiguous() {
+        let err = apply_search_replace("x\nx\n", "x", "y").unwrap_err();
+        assert!(err.to_string().contains("2 locations"));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn search_replace_empty_search() {
+        let err = apply_search_replace("abc", "", "y").unwrap_err();
+        assert!(err.to_string().contains("must not be empty"));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn search_replace_all_counts() {
+        let (out, n) = apply_search_replace_all("x x x", "x", "y").unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(out, "y y y");
+        assert_eq!(n, 3);
+        let err = apply_search_replace_all("abc", "", "y").unwrap_err();
+        assert!(err.to_string().contains("must not be empty"));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn balance_valid_content_is_unchanged() {
+        let mut lang = RustLanguage::new();
+        let out = balance_content("fn main() {}\n", &mut lang, 3).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(
+            out,
+            BalanceOutcome {
+                repaired: "fn main() {}\n".to_string(),
+                actions: 0,
+                cost: 0,
+            }
+        );
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn balance_fixes_missing_paren() {
+        let mut lang = PythonLanguage::new();
+        let out = balance_content("x = (1 + 2\n", &mut lang, 3).unwrap();
+        // The closer lands before the trailing newline.
+        assert_eq!(out.repaired, "x = (1 + 2)\n");
+        assert_eq!(out.actions, 1);
+        assert_eq!(out.cost, 1);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn balance_non_delimiter_error_bails() {
+        let mut lang = PythonLanguage::new();
+        // "def 1bad():" is a syntax error tree-sitter cannot express as a
+        // pure delimiter problem.
+        let err = balance_content("def 1bad():\n", &mut lang, 3).unwrap_err();
+        assert!(err.to_string().contains("not delimiter problems"));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn balance_max_cost_exceeded_bails() {
+        let mut lang = RustLanguage::new();
+        let err = balance_content("fn main() {\n", &mut lang, 0).unwrap_err();
+        assert!(err.to_string().contains("max_cost 0"));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn validate_reports_valid_and_invalid() {
+        let mut lang = RustLanguage::new();
+        let ok = validate_content("fn main() {}\n", &mut lang);
+        assert!(ok.valid);
+        assert_eq!(ok.delimiter_errors.len(), 0);
+
+        let bad = validate_content("fn main() { let x = (1 + 2)); }", &mut lang);
+        assert!(!bad.valid);
+        assert_ne!(bad.delimiter_errors.len(), 0);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn syntax_report_constructors_cover_variants() {
+        // Exercises DelimiterError construction paths used by api consumers
+        // building reports in tests.
+        let extra = DelimiterError::Extra {
+            span: Span {
+                start_byte: 0,
+                end_byte: 1,
+                start_line: 1,
+                start_column: 1,
+                end_line: 1,
+                end_column: 2,
+            },
+            delimiter: ')',
+        };
+        assert_eq!(extra.delta(), -1);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn language_from_extension_roundtrip() {
+        let mut lang = language_from_extension("rs").unwrap();
+        let report = validate_content("fn main() {}\n", &mut *lang);
+        assert!(report.valid);
+
+        let Err(err) = language_from_extension("exotic") else {

+        };

+    }

+/// Harvest tree-sitter `MISSING` delimiter nodes as repairable errors.
+///
+/// When tree-sitter recovers from an unclosed delimiter it inserts a virtual
+/// `MISSING` token at the exact byte position where the closer belongs. These
+/// are invisible to `is_error()` but are precisely the repairs `balance`
+/// exists to make.
+fn collect_missing_delimiters(
+    node: Node,
+    source: &str,
+    errors: &mut Vec<DelimiterError>,
+    index: &LineIndex,
+) {
+    if node.is_missing() {
+        let kind = node.kind();
+        if let Some(ch) = kind.chars().next()
+            && matches!(ch, '(' | '[' | '{' | ')' | ']' | '}')
+            && kind.chars().count() == 1
+        {
+            let mut pos = node.start_byte();
+            // Avoid appending the closer after a trailing newline at EOF:
+            // `x = (1 + 2\n` should get `)` on the same line, and unclosed
+            // blocks get their `}` on a line of its own instead of after `\n`.
+            while pos > 0 && source.as_bytes()[pos - 1] == b'\n' && source[pos..].trim().is_empty()
+            {
+                pos -= 1;
+            }
+            errors.push(DelimiterError::Missing {
+                expected: ch,
+                insert_at: Span::from_byte_range(pos, pos, index),
+                parent_kind: node.parent().map(|p| p.kind().to_string()),
+            });
+        }
+    }
+    for i in 0..node.child_count() {
+        if let Some(c) = node.child(i) {
+            collect_missing_delimiters(c, source, errors, index);
+        }
+    }
+}
+

+    #[must_use]
+    ///
+    /// # Panics
+    ///
+    /// Panics if an internal `unwrap` fails (invariant violation).
+    pub fn new() -> Self {
+        let mut parser = Parser::new();
+        parser
+            .set_language(&tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE.into())
+            .unwrap();
+        Self { parser }
+    }
+    pub fn parser_mut(&mut self) -> &mut Parser {
+        &mut self.parser
+    }
+
+    #[must_use]
+    /// Locate the byte range of the function named `function_name`.
+    ///
+    /// Uses the parsed syntax tree instead of brace counting so that
+    /// *unbalanced* functions (the exact cases `balance` exists to repair)
+    /// are still located, and duplicate definitions are treated as ambiguous
+    /// (`None`).
+    pub fn find_function_body_range(
+        &self,
+        source: &str,
+        function_name: &str,
+    ) -> Option<(usize, usize)> {
+        let mut parser = Parser::new();
+        parser
+            .set_language(&tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE.into())
+            .ok()?;
+        let tree = parser.parse(source, None)?;
+        let root = tree.root_node();
+        // Exact lookup: duplicate definitions are ambiguous.
+        let mut matches: Vec<(usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
+        collect_function_ranges(root, source.as_bytes(), function_name, &mut matches);
+        if matches.len() == 1 {
+            return matches.into_iter().next();
         }
-        None
+        if matches.len() > 1 {
+            return None;
+        }
+        // Fallback: an unbalanced body (the cases `balance` exists to repair)
+        // never forms a `function_item` node. Use the top-level ERROR node
+        // containing its declaration instead, if unique — the root itself may
+        // be that ERROR node when the whole file fails to structure.
+        let pattern = format!("fn {function_name}(");
+        let contains_decl = |n: Node<'_>| {
+            n.utf8_text(source.as_bytes())
+                .is_ok_and(|t| t.contains(&pattern))
+        };
+        let error_ranges: Vec<(usize, usize)> = (0..root.child_count())
+            .filter_map(|i| root.child(i))
+            .chain(std::iter::once(root))
+            .filter(tree_sitter::Node::is_error)
+            .filter(|c| contains_decl(*c))
+            .map(|c| (c.start_byte(), c.end_byte()))
+            .collect();
+        if error_ranges.len() == 1 {
+            error_ranges.into_iter().next()
+        } else {
+            None
+        }
+    }
+}

+fn apply_fixed_diff(

+/// Content-based replacement without a line hint: finds `old` anywhere in the
+/// file (exact first, then relaxed) and replaces it with `new`.
+///
+/// # Errors
+///
+/// Returns an error if the match cannot be found or the patch would introduce
+/// a syntax error.
+fn patch_content_based(file_path: &Path, old: &str, new: &str, args: &PatchArgs) -> Result<()> {
+    let file_content = std::fs::read_to_string(file_path)?;
+    let (byte_range, relaxed) = match crate::matching_flex::find_content_block(&file_content, old) {
+        Some(r) => (r, false),
+        None => match crate::matching_flex::find_content_block_relaxed(&file_content, old) {
+            Some(r) => (r, true),
+            None => anyhow::bail!(
+                "Could not find a match for the provided old content. \
+                     Use a more unique search block or try --symbol."
+            ),
+        },
+    };
+    if relaxed {
+        eprintln!("Warning: exact match not found; used relaxed matching.");
+    }
+    let mut patched = file_content.clone();
+    crate::patch::replace_byte_range(&mut patched, byte_range, new);
+    if !args.dry_run {
+        std::fs::write(file_path, &patched)?;
+    }
+    if !args.force {
+        let mut lang = crate::ast::detect_language(file_path)?;
+        let parse_result = lang.parse(&patched);
+        if !lang.is_valid(&parse_result) {
+            std::fs::write(file_path, &file_content)?;
+            anyhow::bail!(
+                "Content\u{2011}based patch introduced syntax error. \
+                 Use --force to bypass or correct the replacement content."
+            );
         }
-        let manager = HistoryManager::new();
-        manager.save(&file_path.to_string_lossy(), &original, &patched)?;
     }
-    if !args.no_provenance {
-        let record = crate::provenance::ProvenanceRecord::new(
-            &file_path.to_string_lossy(), "patch", args.agent.as_deref(), args.model.as_deref(),
-            serde_json::json!({"syntax": true, "compile": true})
-        );
-        crate::provenance::emit_record(&record)?;
+    Ok(())
+}

+/// Post-patch verification: rolls the patch back when any verification stage
+/// fails, reporting the recall id for later learning.
+///
+/// # Errors
+///
+/// Returns an error if verification fails or the rollback cannot be written.
+///
+/// # Panics
+///
+/// Panics if an internal `unwrap` fails (invariant violation).
+fn enforce_post_verification(
+    file_path: &Path,
+    original: &str,
+    patched: &str,
+    args: &PatchArgs,
+    lang: &mut dyn Language,
+) -> Result<()> {
+    let lang_str = file_path.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str()).unwrap_or("");
+    let verification = crate::verify::verify_patch(
+        file_path,
+        patched,
+        original,
+        lang_str,
+        args.compile_timeout,
+        args.verify_test.as_deref(),
+    )
+    .unwrap_or(crate::verify::VerificationResult {
+        syntax: None,
+        lsp: None,
+        compile: None,
+        tests: None,
+        all_passed: false,
+    });
+    if verification.all_passed {
+        return Ok(());
     }
+    std::fs::write(file_path, original)?;
+    let mut query = crate::recall::RecallQuery::new(
+        file_path,
+        args.line.unwrap_or(1),
+        args.old.as_deref().unwrap_or(""),
+        args.new.as_deref().unwrap_or(""),
+        "E006",
+    );
+    query.error_message = Some("Verification failed");
+    let recall_ctx = crate::recall::generate_recall_context(&query, lang)
+        .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("Failed to generate recall: {e}"));
+    if args.json {
+        let output = serde_json::json!({
+            "patch_applied": false,
+            "verification": verification,
+            "recall_id": recall_ctx.recall_id
+        });
+        println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&output)?);
+    } else {
+        eprintln!("Patch verification failed. Rollback applied.");
+        eprintln!("Recall ID: {}", recall_ctx.recall_id);
+    }
+    anyhow::bail!("Post‑patch verification failed")
+}

-        let current = std::fs::read_to_string(file_path)?;
-        match crate::comment_preserve::preserve_comments(&original, &current, &mut *lang) {
-            Ok(preserved) => { if !args.dry_run { std::fs::write(file_path, &preserved)?; } }
-            Err(e) => eprintln!("Warning: failed to preserve comments: {}", e),
-        }

+        "patch",
+        args.agent.as_deref(),
+        args.model.as_deref(),
+        serde_json::json!({"syntax": true, "compile": true}),
+    );
+    crate::provenance::emit_record(&record)
+}

+/// Detect call sites of functions renamed by the patch.
+fn collect_cross_file_warnings(args: &PatchArgs) -> Vec<String> {
+    let Some(old) = args.old.as_deref() else {
+        return Vec::new();
+    };
+    let Some(new) = args.new.as_deref() else {
+        return Vec::new();
+    };
+    if old == new {
+        return Vec::new();
+    }
+    let Some(old_name) = old.split("fn ").nth(1).and_then(|s| s.split('(').next()) else {
+        return Vec::new();
+    };
+    let Some(new_name) = new.split("fn ").nth(1).and_then(|s| s.split('(').next()) else {
+        return Vec::new();
+    };
+    if old_name == new_name {
+        return Vec::new();
     }
+    let project_index = crate::crossfile::build_project_index(Path::new("."));
+    crate::crossfile::find_callers(old_name, &project_index)
+        .into_iter()
+        .map(|caller| {
+            format!(
+                "Function '{old_name}' was renamed to '{new_name}'. Caller found in {} at line {}",
+                caller.file, caller.line
+            )
+        })
+        .collect()
+}

+/// Build the compiler invocation for a given language.
+/// For Rust we prefer a lock-free single-file `rustc` check and only fall back
+/// to `cargo check` (with an explicit manifest path) when the file belongs to a
+/// cargo project — running bare `cargo check` in the process' cwd can deadlock
+/// on the target-directory lock.
+/// `sink_dir` receives compiler artifacts (rustc metadata) for languages that
+/// need a real output path.
+fn compiler_command(
+    file_path: &Path,
+    lang: &str,
+    sink_dir: &Path,
+) -> Option<(String, Vec<String>)> {
+    let path_str = file_path.to_str()?.to_string();
+    match lang {
+        "rs" => {
+            if let Some(manifest) = find_cargo_manifest(file_path) {
+                Some((
+                    "cargo".into(),
+                    vec![
+                        "check".into(),
+                        "--message-format=short".into(),
+                        "--quiet".into(),
+                        format!("--manifest-path={}", manifest.display()),
+                    ],
+                ))
+            } else {

+                Some((
+                    "rustc".into(),
+                    vec![
+                        "--edition=2021".into(),
+                        "--crate-type=lib".into(),
+                        "--emit=metadata".into(),
+                        "--error-format=short".into(),
+                        "--cap-lints=allow".into(),
+                        "-o".into(),
+                        sink.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
+                        path_str,
+                    ],
+                ))
+            }
+        }
+        "ts" | "tsx" => Some(("tsc".into(), vec!["--noEmit".into(), path_str])),
+        "js" | "jsx" | "mjs" | "cjs" => Some(("node".into(), vec!["--check".into(), path_str])),
+        "py" | "pyi" => Some((
+            "python3".into(),
+            vec!["-m".into(), "py_compile".into(), path_str],
+        )),
+        "go" => Some((

+            vec!["build".into(), "-o".into(), "/dev/null".into(), path_str],
+        )),
+        _ => None,

+/// Resolve the directory a compiler should run in: the file's parent when it
+/// has one, else the current directory (bare relative filenames yield an
+/// empty parent, and `current_dir("")` fails with ENOENT).
+fn working_dir_for(file_path: &Path) -> &Path {
+    file_path
+        .parent()
+        .filter(|p| !p.as_os_str().is_empty())
+        .unwrap_or_else(|| Path::new("."))
+}
+
+/// Drain a child's output stream on a helper thread so the pipe buffer can
+/// never fill up and deadlock the compiler while we wait for it to exit.
+fn drain_stream<R: Read + Send + 'static>(mut stream: R) -> mpsc::Receiver<Vec<u8>> {
+    let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
+    std::thread::spawn(move || {
+        let mut buf = Vec::new();
+        let _ = stream.read_to_end(&mut buf);
+        let _ = tx.send(buf);
+    });
+    rx
+}

+        let mut graph = CallGraph::new();
+        // leaf <- middle <- top: transitive callers chain through the
+        // enclosing function name.
+        graph.add_edge("middle.rs".into(), "middle".into(), 2, 1, "leaf".into());
+        graph.add_edge("top.rs".into(), "top".into(), 7, 2, "middle".into());
+        assert_eq!(graph.all_callers_recursive("leaf").len(), 2);
+        assert_eq!(graph.all_callers_recursive("middle").len(), 1);
+    }

+        graph.add_edge("b.rs".into(), "b_fn".into(), 1, 1, "a".into());
+        // Must terminate despite the cycle.
+        assert!(!graph.all_callers_recursive("a").is_empty());

+        assert!(result.success);

+/// The list of resources advertised over MCP `resources/list`.
+fn resource_list() -> Value {
+    json!([
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://symbols/main.rs","name":"Symbols"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://history","name":"Patch history"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://provenance","name":"Provenance records"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://dashboard","name":"Dashboard","mimeType":"text/html"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://review","name":"Code Review","mimeType":"text/html"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://entities/{file}","name":"Entity listing"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://impact/{symbol}?recursive=true","name":"Call graph lookup"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://semdiff/{file}","name":"Semantic diff"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://coverage/{file}","name":"Coverage report"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://gate/{file}","name":"Gate results"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://score/{file}","name":"Score card"},
+        {"uri":"patch-ts://provenance/{file}","name":"Provenance for file"},
+    ])
+}

+/// Render the text contents of an MCP `resources/read` request.
+fn read_resource(uri: &str, id: &Value) -> Value {
+    match uri {
+        "patch-ts://symbols/main.rs" => {
+            if let Ok(source) = std::fs::read_to_string("main.rs") {
+                crate::symbols::build_index(&source, "rs")
+                    .map(|idx| json!(idx))
+                    .unwrap_or(json!({}))
+            } else {
+                json!({"error":"File not found"})
+            }
+        }
+        "patch-ts://history" => {
+            let mgr = crate::history::HistoryManager::new();
+            json!(mgr.list().unwrap_or_default())
+        }
+        "patch-ts://provenance" => {
+            json!(crate::provenance::query_provenance(None, None).unwrap_or_default())
+        }
+        "patch-ts://dashboard" | "patch-ts://review" => {
+            let state = crate::dashboard::DashboardState {
+                patch_id: "latest".to_string(),
+                diff: None,
+                score: None,
+                provenance: None,
+                gate_result: None,
+                suggestions: None,
+            };
+            let html = crate::dashboard::render_dashboard(&state);
+            json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":id,"result":{"contents":[{"uri":uri,"text":html,"mimeType":"text/html"}]}})
+        }
+        uri if uri.starts_with("patch-ts://entities/") => {
+            let file = uri
+                .strip_prefix("patch-ts://entities/")
+                .unwrap_or("main.rs");
+            if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(file) {
+                if let Ok(mut lang) = crate::ast::detect_language(std::path::Path::new(file)) {
+                    let parse_result = lang.parse(&content);
+                    let entities = lang.find_all_entities(&parse_result);
+                    json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":id,"result":{"contents":[{"uri":uri,"text":serde_json::to_string(&entities).unwrap_or_default(),"mimeType":"application/json"}]}})
+                } else {
+                    json!({"error":"Language not detected"})
+                }
+            } else {
+                json!({"error":"File not found"})
+            }
+        }
+        uri if uri.starts_with("patch-ts://impact/") => {
+            let symbol = uri.strip_prefix("patch-ts://impact/").unwrap_or("");
+            let graph = crate::crossfile::build_project_call_graph(std::path::Path::new("."));
+            let edges = graph.callers_of(symbol);
+            let result: Vec<_> = edges.iter().map(|e| json!({"file": e.caller_file, "line": e.caller_line, "column": e.caller_column})).collect();
+            json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":id,"result":{"contents":[{"uri":uri,"text":serde_json::to_string(&result).unwrap_or_default(),"mimeType":"application/json"}]}})
+        }
+        _ => json!({"error":"Unknown resource"}),
+    }
+}

+fn handle_explain_tool(args: &Value) -> Value {

+fn handle_impact_tool(args: &Value) -> Value {
+    let symbol = args.get("symbol").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
+    let recursive = args
+        .get("recursive")
+        .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_bool)
+        .unwrap_or(false);
+    let graph = crate::crossfile::build_project_call_graph(std::path::Path::new("."));
+    let edges = if recursive {
+        graph.all_callers_recursive(symbol)
+    } else {
+        graph.callers_of(symbol)
+    };
+    let result: Vec<_> = edges
+        .iter()
+        .map(|e| json!({"file": e.caller_file, "line": e.caller_line, "column": e.caller_column}))
+        .collect();
+    json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":serde_json::to_string(&result).unwrap_or_default()}]}})

+fn handle_semdiff_tool(args: &Value) -> Value {
+    let old = args.get("old").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("");

+    let lang = args.get("lang").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("rs");
+    let changes = crate::semdiff::compute_semantic_diff(old, new, lang);
+    let result = serde_json::to_string(&changes).unwrap_or_default();
+    json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":result}]}})

+fn handle_entity_list_tool(args: &Value) -> Value {
+    let file = args

+        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())

+    if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(file)

+    {
+        let parse_result = lang.parse(&content);
+        let entities = lang.find_all_entities(&parse_result);
+        return json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":serde_json::to_string(&entities).unwrap_or_default()}]}});
+    }

+fn handle_entity_replace_tool(args: &Value) -> Value {
+    let file = args
+        .get("file")
+        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
+        .unwrap_or("main.rs");
+    let symbol = args.get("symbol").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
+    let new = args.get("new").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
+    if let Ok(mut lang) = crate::ast::detect_language(std::path::Path::new(file)) {
+        let options = crate::patch::PatchOptions::default();
+        match crate::patch::apply_symbol_patch(
+            std::path::Path::new(file),
+            symbol,
+            new,
+            &options,
+            &mut *lang,
+        ) {
+            Ok(()) => {
+                json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"Entity replaced."}]}})
+            }
+            Err(e) => {
+                json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":format!("Error: {}",e)}],"isError":true}})
+            }
+        }
+    } else {
+        json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"Unsupported file"}],"isError":true}})
+    }
+}
+fn handle_gate_tool(args: &Value) -> Value {
+    let file = args
+        .get("file")
+        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
+        .unwrap_or("main.rs");
+    let stages_str = args
+        .get("stages")
+        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
+        .unwrap_or("syntax,compile");
+    let stages: Vec<String> = stages_str
+        .split(',')
+        .map(|s| s.trim().to_string())
+        .filter(|s| !s.is_empty())
+        .collect();
+    if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(file) {
+        match crate::gate::run_gate(&stages, std::path::Path::new(file), &content, &content, 30) {
+            Ok(res) => {
+                json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":serde_json::to_string(&res).unwrap_or_default()}]}})
+            }
+            Err(e) => {
+                json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":format!("Error: {}",e)}],"isError":true}})
+            }
+        }
+    } else {
+        json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"File not found"}],"isError":true}})
+    }

+        assert!(tools.iter().all(|t| t["inputSchema"].is_object()));

+        assert!(
+            response["error"]["message"]
+                .as_str()
+                .unwrap_or("")
+                .contains("definitely_not_a_tool")
+        );
+    }

+    }

+        let dir = tempdir().unwrap();
+        let file = dir.path().join("sample.rs");
+        fs::write(&file, "fn main() {\n    old_line();\n}\n").unwrap();
+        let response = call(
+            "patch",
+            &json!({"file": file.to_str().unwrap(), "line": 2, "old": "old_line();", "new": "new_line();"}),
+        );
+        assert!(
+            response["result"]["isError"].is_null() && response["error"].is_null(),
+            "patch tool should succeed: {response}"
+        );
+        let content = fs::read_to_string(&file).unwrap();
+        assert!(content.contains("new_line();"));
+    }

+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    use super::*;
+    use std::fs;
+    use tempfile::tempdir;
+
+    fn write_policy(dir: &std::path::Path, extra: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
+        let path = dir.join("policy.toml");
+        fs::write(&path, extra).unwrap();
+        path
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn load_policy_parses_toml() {
+        let dir = tempdir().unwrap();
+        let path = write_policy(
+            dir.path(),
+            "[policy]\nallowed_tools = [\"patch\"]\nmax_patch_size = 100\n",
+        );
+        let policy = load_policy(&path).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(
+            policy.policy.allowed_tools.as_deref(),
+            Some(&["patch".to_string()][..])
+        );
+        assert_eq!(policy.policy.max_patch_size, Some(100));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn load_policy_missing_file_errors() {
+        let dir = tempdir().unwrap();
+        assert!(load_policy(&dir.path().join("nope.toml")).is_err());
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn validate_tool_call_allows_permitted_tool() {
+        let dir = tempdir().unwrap();
+        let path = write_policy(dir.path(), "[policy]\nallowed_tools = [\"patch\"]\n");
+        let policy = load_policy(&path).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(
+            validate_tool_call("patch", None, 10, &policy).unwrap(),
+            None
+        );
+        let denied = validate_tool_call("delete-all", None, 10, &policy).unwrap();
+        assert!(denied.is_some());
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn validate_tool_call_blocks_patterns() {
+        let dir = tempdir().unwrap();
+        let path = write_policy(dir.path(), "[policy]\nblocked_patterns = [\"danger\"]\n");
+        let policy = load_policy(&path).unwrap();
+        let violation = validate_tool_call("very-danger-tool", None, 0, &policy).unwrap();
+        assert!(violation.unwrap().contains("blocked pattern"));
+    }
+

+        assert!(violation.unwrap().contains("allowed paths"));
+    }

+//! Saturating numeric conversion helpers.
+//!
+//! Clippy's pedantic cast lints (rightly) flag `as` casts that may truncate,
+//! wrap or lose precision. Every conversion in this crate goes through these
+//! helpers instead, which either convert losslessly or saturate at the type
+//! boundary. For this crate's domain (line numbers, byte offsets, column
+//! indices) the saturation branch is unreachable in practice, but it keeps the
+//! semantics explicit instead of implicit in a raw cast.
+
+/// Convert a `usize` count to `f64` for ratio/score computations.
+/// Values beyond `u32::MAX` saturate (lossless conversion below that).
+#[must_use]
+pub fn to_f64(n: usize) -> f64 {
+    f64::from(u32::try_from(n).unwrap_or(u32::MAX))
+}
+
+/// Narrow a `usize` (byte offsets, tree-sitter points) to `u32`, saturating.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn to_u32(n: usize) -> u32 {
+    u32::try_from(n).unwrap_or(u32::MAX)
+}
+
+/// Widen a `u64` to `usize`, saturating at `usize::MAX` on 32-bit targets.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn usize_from_u64(n: u64) -> usize {
+    usize::try_from(n).unwrap_or(usize::MAX)
+}
+
+/// Convert a `usize` to `isize`, saturating at `isize::MAX`.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn to_isize(n: usize) -> isize {
+    isize::try_from(n).unwrap_or(isize::MAX)
+}
+
+/// Convert an `isize` to `usize`, saturating negatives to 0.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn to_usize(n: isize) -> usize {
+    usize::try_from(n).unwrap_or(0)
+}
+
+/// Convert an `i64` to `usize`, saturating negatives to 0.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn usize_from_i64(n: i64) -> usize {
+    usize::try_from(n).unwrap_or(0)
+}
+
+/// Convert a `u64` to `u32`, saturating.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn to_u32_from_u64(n: u64) -> u32 {
+    u32::try_from(n).unwrap_or(u32::MAX)
+}
+
+/// Signed difference of two `usize` values, saturating at `isize` bounds.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn isize_diff(a: usize, b: usize) -> isize {
+    to_isize(a).saturating_sub(to_isize(b))
+}
+
+/// Convert a `usize` to `u8`, saturating at `u8::MAX`.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn to_u8(n: usize) -> u8 {
+    u8::try_from(n).unwrap_or(u8::MAX)
+}
+
+/// Round a 0..=1 ratio to a 0..=100 percent, clamping out-of-range input.
+///
+/// Avoids a raw `f64 as u8` cast (clippy rightly flags those as truncating):
+/// after rounding and clamping the value is an integer by construction, so it
+/// is round-tripped through its decimal representation instead.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn percent(x: f64) -> u8 {
+    if !x.is_finite() {
+        return 0;
+    }
+    let scaled = (x * 100.0).round().clamp(0.0, 100.0);
+    format!("{scaled}").parse::<u8>().unwrap_or(0)
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    use super::*;
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_to_u8_saturates() {
+        assert_eq!(to_u8(99), 99);
+        assert_eq!(to_u8(usize::MAX), u8::MAX);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_percent_rounds_and_clamps() {
+        assert_eq!(percent(1.0), 100);
+        assert_eq!(percent(0.0), 0);
+        assert_eq!(percent(0.123_4), 12);
+        assert_eq!(percent(0.999), 100);
+        assert_eq!(percent(-5.0), 0);
+        assert_eq!(percent(42.0), 100);
+        assert_eq!(percent(f64::NAN), 0);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_isize_diff() {
+        assert_eq!(isize_diff(10, 4), 6);
+        assert_eq!(isize_diff(4, 10), -6);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_to_f64_lossless_small() {
+        assert_eq!(to_f64(42), 42.0);
+        assert_eq!(to_f64(0), 0.0);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_to_f64_saturates_beyond_u32() {
+        assert_eq!(to_f64(usize::MAX), f64::from(u32::MAX));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_to_u32_saturates() {
+        assert_eq!(to_u32(1_000), 1_000);
+        assert_eq!(to_u32(usize::MAX), u32::MAX);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_usize_from_u64_saturates() {
+        assert_eq!(usize_from_u64(7), 7);
+        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "32")]
+        assert_eq!(usize_from_u64(u64::MAX), usize::MAX);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_to_isize_and_back() {
+        assert_eq!(to_isize(9), 9);
+        assert_eq!(to_isize(usize::MAX), isize::MAX);
+        assert_eq!(to_usize(-3), 0);
+        assert_eq!(to_usize(11), 11);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_usize_from_i64_saturates_negatives() {
+        assert_eq!(usize_from_i64(-1), 0);
+        assert_eq!(usize_from_i64(500), 500);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_to_u32_from_u64_saturates() {
+        assert_eq!(to_u32_from_u64(u64::MAX), u32::MAX);
+        assert_eq!(to_u32_from_u64(3), 3);
+    }
+}

+/// Locate the target of the patch: fuzzy block/line matching when allowed,
+/// otherwise the exact line.
+///
+/// # Errors
+///
+/// Returns an error if no sufficiently similar match can be found.
+fn locate_match_target(
+    lines: &[&str],
+    line_num: usize,
+    expected: &str,
+    target_idx: usize,
+    options: &mut PatchOptions,
+    original_content: &str,
+) -> Result<(usize, Option<String>)> {
+    if options.fuzz_radius == 0 {
+        if lines[target_idx] != expected {
+            anyhow::bail!(
+                "expected line {} to contain '{}', but found '{}'",
+                line_num,
+                expected,
+                lines[target_idx]
+            );
+        }
+        return Ok((target_idx, None));
+    }
+    if expected.contains('\n') {
+        let block_match = find_best_block_match(
+            lines,
+            expected,
+            options.fuzz_radius,
+            options.similarity_threshold,
+        )?;
+        Ok((block_match.start_index, None))
+    } else {
+        let line_match = cascade_match(
+            lines,
+            line_num,
+            expected,
+            options.fuzz_radius,
+            options.confidence_threshold,
+            options.uniqueness_weight,
+            original_content,
+        )?;
+        options.match_info = Some(line_match.clone());
+        Ok((line_match.index, Some(lines[line_match.index].to_string())))
+    }
+}
+
+/// Reject (or auto-repair) patches that turn valid syntax into invalid syntax.
+///
+/// # Errors
+///
+/// Returns an error with a syntax diagnostic when the patch cannot be repaired.
+fn enforce_syntax_gate(
+    original_content: &str,
+    mut new_content: String,
+    file_path: &Path,
+    options: &PatchOptions,
+    language: &mut dyn Language,
+) -> Result<String> {
+    let original_parse = language.parse(original_content);
+    let was_valid = language.is_valid(&original_parse);
+    let new_parse = language.parse(&new_content);
+    let is_valid = language.is_valid(&new_parse);
+    if was_valid && !is_valid {
+        if options.match_behavior.no_auto_repair {
+            return Err(syntax_error_diag(&new_content, file_path, language, &new_parse).into());
+        }
+        if let Some(fixed_content) = crate::repair::quick_balance(&new_content, language) {
+            eprintln!("Warning: Patch introduced syntax error but was auto-repaired.");
+            new_content = fixed_content;
+        } else {
+            return Err(syntax_error_diag(&new_content, file_path, language, &new_parse).into());
+        }
+    }
+    Ok(new_content)
+}
+
+/// Build a rich syntax-error diagnostic for the patched content.
+fn syntax_error_diag(
+    new_content: &str,
+    file_path: &Path,
+    language: &mut dyn Language,
+    new_parse: &crate::ast::ParseResult,
+) -> SyntaxErrorDiagnostic {
+    language
+        .explain_error(new_parse, 1)
+        .unwrap_or_else(|| SyntaxErrorDiagnostic {
+            src: NamedSource::new(file_path.to_string_lossy(), new_content.to_string()),
+            error_span: (0, 0).into(),
+            details: "Unknown syntax error".to_string(),
+        })
+}

 // --- Core recall generation ---
+///
+/// # Errors
+///
+/// Returns an error if the file or record cannot be read.
+/// Everything the recall-context generator needs to know about a failure.
+#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
+pub struct RecallQuery<'a> {
+    pub file_path: &'a Path,
+    pub line: usize,
+    pub old_content: &'a str,
+    pub new_content: &'a str,
+    pub error_code: &'a str,
+    pub error_message: Option<&'a str>,
+    pub context_lines: usize,
+    pub use_entropy: bool,
+    pub entropy_threshold: f64,
+    pub pre_fetch: bool,
+    pub session_id: Option<&'a str>,
+    pub max_tokens: Option<usize>,
+}
+
+impl<'a> RecallQuery<'a> {
+    /// Minimal query with sensible defaults (no entropy, no session).
+    #[must_use]
+    pub fn new(
+        file_path: &'a Path,
+        line: usize,
+        old_content: &'a str,
+        new_content: &'a str,
+        error_code: &'a str,
+    ) -> Self {
+        Self {
+            file_path,
+            line,
+            old_content,
+            new_content,
+            error_code,
+            error_message: None,
+            context_lines: 5,
+            use_entropy: false,
+            entropy_threshold: 2.5,
+            pre_fetch: false,
+            session_id: None,
+            max_tokens: None,
+        }
+    }
+}
+

 /// that makes the source syntactically valid according to the language's parser.
 ///
+/// `initial_errors` are the delimiter errors already computed for `source` by
+/// the caller; they seed the search frontier so the first expansion needs no
+/// extra parse. If the caller's errors turn out to be false positives (the
+/// source parses cleanly), the source is returned unchanged at cost 0.
+///

+            let mut hasher = std::collections::hash_map::DefaultHasher::new();
+            state.source.hash(&mut hasher);
+            if visited.insert(hasher.finish()) {
+                queue.push_back(state);

+    // initial state is known to be invalid, so there is nothing to re-parse.
+    let initial_state = RepairState::new(source.to_string());
+    for error in initial_errors {
+        for next_state in generate_next_states(&initial_state, error) {
+            push_if_new(next_state, &mut queue, &mut visited);
+        }
+    }
+

+        // Solutions more expensive than the budget are never accepted, no
+        // matter that they parse cleanly.
+        if state.cost > max_cost {
+            continue;

+    #[test]
+    fn test_detect_bad_signature() {
+        let dir = tempdir().unwrap();
+        let log_path = dir.path().join("provenance.jsonl");
+        let (signing_key, verifying_key) = generate_keypair();
+
+        emit_scitt_record(&log_path, "patch content 1", None, None, Some(&signing_key)).unwrap();
+
+        // Forge a validly-encoded but wrong signature on the single record.
+        let content = fs::read_to_string(&log_path).unwrap();
+        let mut record: ScittRecord =
+            serde_json::from_str(content.lines().next().unwrap()).unwrap();
+        record.signature = Some(hex::encode([0u8; 64]));
+        fs::write(&log_path, serde_json::to_string(&record).unwrap() + "\n").unwrap();
+
+        let tampered = verify_log(&log_path, Some(&verifying_key)).unwrap();
+        assert!(!tampered.is_empty(), "bad signature must be detected");
     }

+/// Compute a semantic diff between two versions of a file (or two strings).
+/// Returns a list of `EntityChange` with body comparison.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn compute_semantic_diff(
+    old_content: &str,
+    new_content: &str,
+    lang: &str,
+) -> Vec<EntityChange> {
+    let old_entities = extract_entities(old_content, lang).unwrap_or_default();
+    let new_entities = extract_entities(new_content, lang).unwrap_or_default();
+    let mut changes = diff_entities(&old_entities, &new_entities);
+    // For Modified/Moved, we can add a simple body diff placeholder.
+    for change in &mut changes {
+        if let ChangeType::Modified = change.change_type {
+            // Provide a diff of the signatures (or a simple string diff)
+            if let Some(old_entity) = &change.old_entity {
+                let old_sig = &old_entity.signature;
+                let new_sig = &change.entity.signature;
+                if old_sig != new_sig {
+                    change.diff = Some(format!("- {old_sig}\n+ {new_sig}"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    changes

+        fs::write(&code, "fn present_fn() {}\n").unwrap();
+        let warnings = validate_spec(&spec, &code).unwrap();

+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    use super::*;
+
+    #[test]
+    fn word_diff_identical_lines_are_unchanged() {
+        let (old, new) = word_diff("hello world", "hello world");
+        assert!(
+            old.iter()
+                .all(|c| matches!(c.change_type, ChangeType::Unchanged))
+        );
+        assert!(
+            new.iter()
+                .all(|c| matches!(c.change_type, ChangeType::Unchanged))
+        );
+        let joined: String = old.iter().map(|c| c.text.as_str()).collect();
+        assert_eq!(joined, "hello world");
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn word_diff_marks_added_and_removed_words() {
+        let (old, new) = word_diff("hello world", "goodbye world");
+        assert!(
+            old.iter()
+                .any(|c| matches!(c.change_type, ChangeType::Removed)),
+            "old side should contain removals"
+        );
+        assert!(
+            new.iter()
+                .any(|c| matches!(c.change_type, ChangeType::Added)),
+            "new side should contain additions"
+        );
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn colorize_word_changes_emits_ansi() {
+        let (_, new) = word_diff("foo bar", "foo baz");
+        let colored = colorize_word_changes(&new);
+        assert_ne!(colored.len(), 0);
+        assert!(colored.contains('\u{1b}'), "expected ANSI escape codes");
+        assert!(colored.contains("foo"));
+    }
+}
